Remark 2.40
.

In our case, the obstruction for lifting Hodge filtration vanish even the lifting is unique due to
subsection 2.1
. In particular, lifting flow is uniquely determined by the lifting
(E,\theta)_{0}
.

Since the Higgs bundle in
{\mathrm{HIG}^{{\rm gr}\,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(S)
is uniquely determined by its zero, the lifting torsor space of
(\overline{E},\overline{\theta})_{0}
is isomorphic to
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}_{k}
(non-canonically). In
[
KYZ20
]
, the operator
\Big(\Gr\circ C_{2}^{-1}\Big)^{f}
induces a self map on this torsor space, which is of form
z\mapsto az^{p}+b
if we choose an identification of the torsor space with the affine line over
k
. In particular, the solutions of the Artin-Schreier equation

(2.7)

az^{p}+b=z

correspond to
f
-periodic Higgs bundles in
{\mathrm{HIG}^{{\rm gr}\,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(S_{2})
which lifts
(\overline{E},\overline{\theta})_{0}
.
Hence, if we extend the field
k
a little bit, we can always find
f
-periodic Higgs bundles in
{\mathrm{HIG}^{{\rm gr}\,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(S_{2})
which lifts
(\overline{E},\overline{\theta})_{0}
.

Remark 2.41
.

If
a=0
, then there are exact one periodic lifting of
(\overline{E},\overline{\theta})_{0}
in
{\mathrm{HIG}^{{\rm gr}\,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(S_{2})
. In this case, we do not need extend the field, the lifting is already defined over
S_{2}
.

If
a\neq 0
, then there are exact
p
periodic lifting of
(\overline{E},\overline{\theta})_{0}
in
{\mathrm{HIG}^{{\rm gr}\,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(S_{2})
, once we enlarge the field
k
a little bit properly.

Lifting over
S
.
Working the above lifting procedure inductively, we obtain
f
-periodic Higgs bundles in
{\mathrm{HIG}^{{\rm gr}\,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(\overline{S})
, which lifts
(\overline{E},\overline{\theta})_{0}
.

Remark 2.42
.

The lifting of a periodic Higgs bundle over
{\mathbb{F}_{q}}
to a periodic Higgs bundle over
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{ur}
is in general not unique, as the solutions of the Artin-Schreier equation are not unique in general.

The associated parabolic Fontaine-Faltings modules.
Let
(E,\theta)_{0}\in{\mathrm{HIG}^{{\rm gr}\,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(\overline{S})
be
f
-periodic with the corresponding flow

\Big((V,\nabla,E^{1,0})_{-1},(E,\theta)_{0},(V,\nabla,E^{1,0})_{0},\cdots(E,\theta)_{f-1},(V,\nabla,E^{1,0})_{f-1},(E,\theta)_{f},\cdots\Big)

Similarly, as in
subsubsection 2.3.2
, we gets a parabolic Fontaine-Faltings module over
({\mathbb{P}}_{\overline{S}}^{1},D_{\overline{S}})/\overline{S}
.

2.5.2.
Lifting in supersingular case

Definition 2.43
.

An element
\lambda
in
{\mathcal{O}}_{S}(S)=W(k)
such that

\overline{\lambda}:=\lambda\pmod{p}\neq 0,1\in k

is called
supersingular
if the elliptic curve

C_{\overline{\lambda}}
is supersingular.

By direct calculation, one can check that if
\lambda
is supersingular, then the coefficient
a
appeared in the Artin-Schreier equation (
2.7
) is zero, for explicit calculation see the
Appendix C
. In particular, one has the following theorem.

Theorem 2.44
.

Assume that
\lambda
is supersingular. Any
f
-periodic parabolic Higgs bundle
(\overline{E},\overline{\theta})\in{\mathrm{PHIG}^{{\rm gr}\,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda,f}}(k)
has a unique
f
-periodic lifting
(E,\theta)\in{\mathrm{PHIG}^{{\rm gr}\,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda,f}}(W(k))
. In other words, the modulo
p
reduction induces a bijection

{\mathrm{PHIG}^{{\rm gr}\,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda,f}}(W(k))\xrightarrow{1:1}{\mathrm{PHIG}^{{\rm gr}\,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda,f}}(k).

3.
Overconvergent
F
-isocrystals

In this section, the aim is to

•

construct a natural injective map (
subsubsection 3.4.2
)

(3.1)

\textstyle{[\mathcal{MF}^{{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}(W(k_{2}^{\prime}))_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p^{f}}}^{\rm cy}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[{\FIsoc^{\dagger{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k^{\prime}_{2})_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{f}}}^{\rm triv}].}

where
[{\FIsoc^{\dagger{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k^{\prime}_{2})_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{f}}}^{\rm triv}]
is the set of all rank-
2
overconvergent
F
-isocrystal over projective line with given exponents and trivial determinant modulo an equivalent relation defined in
subsubsection 3.4.2
, and

•

show that the image of the following composition

(3.2)

\textstyle{{\mathrm{HIG}^{{\rm gr}\,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
2.1
)
\textstyle{[\mathcal{MF}^{{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}(W(k^{\prime}_{2}))_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p^{f}}}^{\rm cy}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[{\FIsoc^{\dagger{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k^{\prime}_{2})_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{f}}}^{\rm triv}]}

is fixed by the Frobenius action, see
subsubsection 3.4.3
.

3.1.
F
-crystals

In this subsection, we recall some basic definitions we need for this article, including those of convergent
F
-isocrystals, overconvergent
F
-isocrystals and convergent log-
F
-isocrystals from
[
Ked22
, Definition 2.1, Definition 2.4 and Definition 7.1]
.

Let
X
be a proper smooth variety over
k
,
D
be a normal crossing divisor in
X
and
U=X-D
. We endow
X
with the natural logarithmic structure induced by
D
, and simply write
(X,D)
for the corresponding logarithmic scheme.

3.1.1.
(logarithmic)
F
-crystal

Kato has defined (logarithmic) crystalline site
((X,D)/W)^{\log}_{\rm crys}
, and
\Crys((X,D)/W)
the category of crystals in
finite coherent
{\mathcal{O}}_{(X,D)/W}
-modules
. By functoriality of the crystalline topos, the absolute Frobenius
\mathrm{Frob}_{X}:X\rightarrow X
gives a functor
\mathrm{Frob}_{X}^{*}\colon\Crys((X,D)/W)\rightarrow\Crys((X,D)/W)
. An
(logarithmic)
F
-crystal
in finite, locally free modules on
U
is a crystal
{\mathcal{E}}
in finite, locally free
{\mathcal{O}}_{(X,D)/W}
-modules together with an isogeny
F\colon\mathrm{Frob}_{X}^{*}{\mathcal{E}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{E}}
. The
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-linear category of (logarithmic)
F
-crystals in finite, locally free modules is denoted as
\FCrys((X,D)/W)
.

Theorem 3.1

(Kato)
.

There is an equivalence between the following two categories:

(1)

the category of crystals
{\mathcal{E}}
on
((X,D)/W)^{\log}_{\rm crys}
,

(2)

the category of
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{X}}}
-modules
V
on
{\mathcal{X}}
with a quasi-nilpotent integrable logarithmic connection

\nabla\colon V\rightarrow V\otimes\Omega^{1}_{{\mathcal{X}}/W}(\log{\mathcal{D}}).

Remark 3.2
.

We call the de Rham sheaf
(V,\nabla)
associated to a crystal
{\mathcal{E}}
to be the
realization of
{\mathcal{E}}
over
({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{D}})
. Kato’s Theorem implies a logarithmic de Rham bundle is a realization of a logarithmic crystal if its connection is quasi-nilpotent. We sometimes simply call such a logarithmic de Rham sheaf
(V,\nabla)
a logarithmic crystal over
(X,D)
.

According
subsubsection 3.1.1
, we also write the logarithmic
F
-crystal as the triple
(V,\nabla,{\mathcal{F}})
.

Corollary 3.3
.

There is an equivalence between the following two categories

(1)

the category of
F
-crystals
{\mathcal{E}}
on
((X,D)/W)^{\log}_{\rm crys}
,

(2)

the category of triples
(V,\nabla,\Phi)
, where
V
is vector bundle on
{\mathcal{X}}
,
\nabla
a quasi-nilpotent integrable logarithmic connection

\nabla\colon V\rightarrow V\otimes\Omega^{1}_{{\mathcal{X}}/W}(\log{\mathcal{D}})

and
\Phi
is an injection

\Phi\colon\varphi^{*}(V,\nabla)\hookrightarrow(V,\nabla).

Corollary 3.4
.

Let
(V,\nabla,\Fil,\Phi)
be a Fontaine-Faltings module. By forgetting the filtration, one gets an
F
-crystal over
(X,D)
.

Remark 3.5
.

For an
F
-crystal over
(X,D)
, consider its realization
(V,\nabla,\Phi)
on the
p
-adic formal completion of
({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{D}})
. The presence of the Frobenius structure forces the reductions modulo
{\mathbb{Z}}
of the eigenvalues of the residue map would form a set stable under multiplication by
p
. In particular the eigenvalues are rational numbers. See
[
Ked22
, 7.2]
.

3.2.
F
-isocrystals

In this subsection, we also recall some basic definitions needed for this article, including those of convergent
F
-isocrystals, overconvergent
F
-isocrystals and convergent log-
F
-isocrystals from
[
Ked22
, Definition 2.1, Definition 2.4 and Definition 7.1]
.

Let
X
be a proper smooth variety over
k
,
D
be a normal crossing divisor in
X
and
U=X-D
. We endow
X
with the natural logarithmic structure induced by
D
, and simply write
(X,D)
for the corresponding logarithmic scheme.

3.2.1.
overconvergent
F
-isocrystal

Suppose there exists a lifting
\sigma\colon{\mathcal{U}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{U}}
of the absolute Frobenius on

U
. A
convergent
F
-isocrystal
over
U
is a de Rham bundle
{\mathcal{E}}
over the Raynaud generic fiber
{\mathcal{U}}_{K}
of the formal completion
{\mathcal{U}}
of
U
along the special fiber
U
together with an isomorphism
F\colon\sigma^{*}{\mathcal{E}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{E}}
of de Rham bundles. Denote by
\FIsoc(U)
the category of all convergent
F
-isocrystals over
U
. Up to canonical equivalence, this category does not depend on the choice of the lifting
\sigma
. In general, there may not exist a global lifting of the absolute Frobenius on
U
, but one can still define the category
\FIsoc(U)
(see
[
Ked22
, definition 2.1]
). One way to do this is as follows: we can find local liftings of absolute Frobenius on
U
, define local categories by using these local liftings as above, and use the canonical equivalences between local categories to glue them into a global one.

A convergent
F
-isocrystal is called
overconvergent
if it can be extended to a strict neighborhood of
{\mathcal{U}}_{K}
in
{\mathcal{X}}_{K}
. Denote by
\FIsoc^{\dagger}(U)
the category of all overconvergent
F
-isocrystal over
U
.

For each finite extension
L
of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
within
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
, let
\FIsoc^{\dagger}(U)_{L}
denote the category of objects of
\FIsoc^{\dagger}(U)
with a
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-linear action of
L
. Let
\FIsoc^{\dagger}(U)_{\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}}
be the
2
-colimit of the category
\FIsoc^{\dagger}(U)_{L}
over all finite extensions
L
of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
within
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
.

3.2.2.
characteristic polynomials of an overconvergent
F
-isocrystal

Given an overconvergent
F
-isocrystal
{\mathcal{E}}
on
U
. For any closed point
x
in
U
, the fiber
{\mathcal{E}}_{x}
of
{\mathcal{E}}
at
x
carries an action of (geometric) Frobenius. We define the characteristic polynomial of
{\mathcal{E}}
at
x
to be

P_{x}({\mathcal{E}},t)=\det(1-Fr_{x}\cdot t\mid_{{\mathcal{E}}_{x}}).

3.2.3.
convergent log-
F
-isocrystal

A
convergent log-
F
-isocrystal
is a logarithmic de Rham bundle over
{\mathcal{X}}_{K}
together with an isomorphism
F
of logarithmic de Rham bundles similar as that in the definition of convergent
F
-isocrystal (see e.g.
[
Ked22
, Definition 7.1]
). For such objects, the residues of the underlying logarithmic isocrystal are automatically nilpotent. We denote by
\FIsoc^{\rm nilp}_{\log}(X,S)
the category of all convergent log-
F
-isocrystals on the logarithmic pair
(X,S)
.

Remark 3.6
.

(1)

Under our assumption
X_{K}
is proper, a convergent log-
F
-isocrystals can be algebraicalized to a vector bundle over
X_{K}
together with an integral logarithmic connection and a parallel semilinear action.

(2)

To a logarithmic crystalline representation, we may attach an convergent log-
F
-isocrystal. For a logarithmic crystalline representation
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(U_{K})\rightarrow\textrm{GL}_{r}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p^{f}})
, according Faltings’ definition of crystalline representation
[
Fal89
]
, there exists an attached logarithmic Fontaine-Faltings module
(V,\nabla,\Fil,\varphi,\iota)
5
5

5

Faltings’ original definition is for
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-representations. It can be easily extended to
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p^{f}}
-representations by adding an endomorphism structures
\iota
on the side of Fontaine-Faltings modules. More precisely, see
[
LSZ19
]
.
Forgetting the filtration and tensoring
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
, one gets the attached convergent log-
F
-isocrystal
(V,\nabla,\varphi,\iota)_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
.

3.2.4.
Trace of the Frobenius

Let
(V,\nabla,\Fil,\varphi)
be a logarithmic Fontaine-Faltings module over
({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{Y}})
.
For any closed point
x
in
U_{1}
with residue field
k^{\prime}
, by the smoothness of
Y
, we can find a
\Spf(W)
-point
\widehat{x}
in
{\mathcal{U}}
which lifts
x
. By restricting on
\widehat{x}
, we gets a Fontaine-Faltings module over this point, which is nothing just a finite generated free filtered
W(k^{\prime})
-module
V_{\widehat{x}}
together with a
\sigma
-semilinear isomorphism
F_{\widehat{x}}\colon\widetilde{V}_{\widehat{x}}\simeq V_{\widehat{x}}
, where
\widetilde{V}_{\widehat{x}}=\sum\limits_{\ell=a}^{b}\frac{1}{p^{\ell}}\Fil^{\ell}V_{\widehat{x}}\subset V_{\widehat{x}}\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
. By tensoring
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
, one gets an
F
-isocrystal
(V_{\widehat{x}}\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}_{p},F_{\widehat{x}})
over the finite field
k^{\prime}
.

One can easily checks following result.

Lemma 3.7
.

The
(V_{\widehat{x}}\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}_{p},F_{\widehat{x}})
is isomorphic to the restriction of
{\mathcal{E}}
on
x
. In particular, the isomorphic class of
F
-isocrystal
(V_{\widehat{x}}\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}_{p},F_{\widehat{x}})
does not depend on the choice of
\widehat{x}
.

3.2.5.
The dependence of the traces on the choices of the Frobenius structures

3.2.6.
F
-isocrystal over
k
with coefficients

Let
k
be a finite field and Let
L
be an algebraic extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
. Recall that the following are equivalent:

•

an
F
-isocrystal over
k
with coefficient in
L
of rank
r
;

•

a free
W(k)[\frac{1}{p}]\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}L
-module of rank
r
together with a
\sigma\otimes\id
-linear morphism

F\colon V\rightarrow V.

•

a
W(k)[\frac{1}{p}]
vector space of rank
r[L:{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}]
endowed with a
\sigma
-semilinear isomorphism
F\colon V\rightarrow V
and with an endomorphism structure

L\rightarrow\End(V,F).

In the following, we will always identify the three kinds of objects, and call them
F
-isocrystals over
k
with coefficient
L
. Denote by
\FIsoc(k)_{L}
the category of all
F
-isocrystals over
k
with coefficient
L
.

3.2.7.
The
F
-isocrystal
{\mathcal{E}}_{1/2}
.

Since
p\geq 3
, we may choose a square root
\sqrt{1-p}
of
1-p
in
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
. Since
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{2}}
is an extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
of degree
2
, we may find some
\zeta\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{2}}\setminus{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
such that
\zeta^{2}\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
. Thus
\sigma(\zeta)=-\zeta
, where
\sigma
is the generator of the Galois group
\Gal({\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{2}}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
, which is also the lifting of the absolute Frobenius map on
{\mathbb{F}}_{p^{2}}
.

Let
V_{1/2}
be a
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{2}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{2}}
-module of rank
1
with basis
e
. Denote by
F_{1/2}
a
\sigma\otimes\id
-linear endomorphism on
V
given by

F_{1/2}(e)=(1\otimes 1+\sqrt{1-p}\zeta\otimes\zeta^{-1})e

Then

F_{1/2}^{2}(e)=(1\otimes 1-\sqrt{1-p}\zeta\otimes\zeta^{-1})\cdot(1\otimes 1+\sqrt{1-p}\zeta\otimes\zeta^{-1})e=p.

According the equivalent relation, we get an
F
-isocrystal,denote by
{\mathcal{E}}_{1/2}
, over
{\mathbb{F}}_{p^{2}}
with coefficient in
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{2}}
.
Let
X
be an varieties defined over
k
. Assume
k
contains
{\mathbb{F}}_{p^{2}}
. Then there is a structure morphism

f\colon X\rightarrow\Spec({\mathbb{F}}_{p^{2}}).

By pulling back
{\mathcal{E}}_{1/2}
along
f
we get a constant overconvergent
F
-isocrystal of rank
1
with coefficient in
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{2}}
. By abusing notion, we still denote it by
{\mathcal{E}}_{1/2}
.

3.2.8.
The cyclotomic
F
-isocrystal
{\mathcal{E}}_{cy}

Definition 3.8
.

Let
V
be a
{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-module of rank
1
with basis
e
. Denote by
F
the
{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-linear endomorphism on
V
by multiplying
p
. Then we get an
F
-isocrystal, denote by
{\mathcal{E}}_{cy}
, over
{\mathbb{F}_{p}}
.

Lemma 3.9
.

{\mathcal{E}}_{cy}={\mathcal{E}}_{1/2}^{\otimes 2}
.

3.2.9.
The change of traces of Frobenius under twisting by
{\mathcal{E}}_{1/2}

Let
k
be a finite field with cardinality
p^{h}
. Let
L
be an algebraic extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.

Let
(V,F)
be an
F
-isocrystal over
k
with coefficient in
L
of rank
r
, or equivalently, a free
W(k)[\frac{1}{p}]\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}L
-module of rank
r
together with a
\sigma\otimes\id
-linear morphism

F\colon V\rightarrow V.

Then
F^{h}
is a
W(k)[\frac{1}{p}]\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}L
-linear endomorphism on
V
. Denote by
P((V,F),t)
the characteristic polynomial and by
\tr(V,F)
the trace of
F^{h}
acting on
V
.

Lemma 3.10
.

P((V,F),t)\in L[t]
and
tr(V,F)\in L
.

Proof.

Let
e_{1},\cdots,e_{r}
be a system
W(k)[\frac{1}{p}]\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}L
-basis of
V
. Then
F
can be represented as

F(e_{1},\cdots,e_{r})=(e_{1},\cdots,e_{r})A.

Thus

F^{h}(e_{1},\cdots,e_{r})=(e_{1},\cdots,e_{r})\underbrace{A\cdot A^{\sigma\otimes\id}\cdot A^{(\sigma\otimes\id)^{2}}\cdot\cdots\cdot A^{(\sigma\otimes\id)^{h-1}}}_{=:B}.

Since
B^{\sigma\otimes\id}=A^{-1}BA
, both
P((V,F),t)
and
tr(V,F)
are invariant under
\sigma\otimes\id
.
∎

Denote by
k^{\prime}
the field generated by
k
and
{\mathbb{F}}_{p^{2}}
and denote by
L^{\prime}
the field generated by
L
and
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{2}}
. Then both

R_{1}:={\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{2}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{2}}
and
R_{2}:=W(k)[\frac{1}{p}]\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}L

can be viewed as a subring of
R:=W(k^{\prime})[\frac{1}{p}]\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}L^{\prime}
.
By extending the ring from
R_{1}
and
R_{2}
to
R
, we gets two objects in
\FIsoc(k^{\prime})_{L^{\prime}}
from
(V,F)
and
{\mathcal{E}}_{1/2}
. We denote by

(V,F)\otimes{\mathcal{E}}_{1/2}

their tensor product in the category
\FIsoc(k^{\prime})_{L^{\prime}}
.

Lemma 3.11
.

Suppose
{\mathbb{F}}_{p^{2}}\subseteq k
. Then
2\mid h
,

P((V,F)\otimes{\mathcal{E}}_{1/2},t)=p^{rh/2}P((V,F),p^{-h/2}t)\quad\text{and}\quad\tr((V,F)\otimes{\mathcal{E}}_{1/2})=p^{h/2}\tr(V,F).

Proof.

Clearly, the surjective
R
-module of
(V,F)\otimes{\mathcal{E}}_{1/2}
is

(V\otimes_{R_{2}}R)\otimes_{R}(V_{1/2}\otimes_{R_{1}}R)

which is free over
R
of rank
r
with generators

e_{1}^{\prime}:=(e_{1}\otimes 1)\otimes(e\otimes 1),\cdots,e^{\prime}_{r}:=(e_{r}\otimes 1)\otimes(e\otimes 1).

Denote
\eta:=1\otimes 1+\sqrt{1-p}\zeta\otimes\zeta^{-1}
. Then

F(e^{\prime}_{1},\cdots e^{\prime}_{r})=(e^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,e^{\prime}_{r})\cdot A\eta.

The Lemma follows the following calculate:

\begin{split}F^{h}(e_{1}\otimes e,\cdots e_{r}\otimes e)&=(e_{1}\otimes e,\cdots e_{r}\otimes e)\cdot A\eta\cdot(A\eta)^{\sigma\otimes\id}\cdot\cdots\cdot(A\eta)^{(\sigma\otimes\id)^{h-1}}\\
&=(e_{1}\otimes e,\cdots e_{r}\otimes e)\cdot p^{h/2}B\\
\end{split}

∎

3.3.
The convergence of parabolic Fontaine-Faltings modules.

In this subsection, we construct the overconvergent
F
-isocrystals from parabolic Fontaine-Faltings modules.

3.3.1.
convergence of a logarithmic de Rham bundle over
({\mathcal{Y}}_{K},{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{K}})

Recall that Kedlaya gave an equivalent functor
[
Ked07
, 6.4.1]
from the category of convergent
logarithmic isocrystals
[
Ked07
, 6.1.7]
to the category of convergent log de Rham bundles
[
Ked07
, 6.3.1]
. So by restricting from the associated convergent logarithmic isocrystal, one gets an overconvergent isocrystal from a convergent logarithmic de Rham bundle. Back to our situation, we only need to show the convergence of the underlying logarithmic de Rham bundle of a logarithmic Fontaine-Faltings module. Before this, let us recall Kedlaya’s definition of convergence.

Definition 3.12

(
[
Ked07
, 6.3.1]
)
.

A logarithmic de Rham bundle
(V,\nabla)
over
({\mathcal{Y}}_{K},{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{K}})
is called
convergent
, if there exists some strict neighborhood of
{\mathcal{U}}_{K}
in
{\mathcal{Y}}_{K}
, on which the restriction of
(V,\nabla)
is overconvergent
6
6

6

See
[
Ked07
, 2.5.3 and 2.5.4]
along
{\mathcal{Z}}_{K}
.

Remark 3.13
.

According
[
Ked07
, Proposition 2.5.6]
, a logarithmic de Rham bundle over
({\mathcal{Y}}_{K},{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{K}})
is convergent if and only if for any
\eta\in[0,1)
, there exists a sufficient small strict neighborhood of
{\mathcal{U}}_{K}
in
{\mathcal{Y}}_{K}
, on which the restriction is
\eta
-convergent
7
7

7

See the explicit definition for
\eta
-convergent in
[
Ked07
, Definition 2.4.2]
.

3.3.2.
Generic fiber of a logarithmic de Rham bundle over
({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{Y}})

Let
(V,\nabla)
be logarithmic de Rham bundle over
({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{Y}})
. By restriction on the Raynaud generic fiber, one gets a logarithmic de Rham bundle
({\mathcal{Y}}_{K},{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{K}})
, which we will simply call
the generic fiber of
(V,\nabla)
, and denote by
(V_{K},\nabla_{K})
.

Lemma 3.14
.

Let
(V,\nabla,\Fil,\varphi)
be a parabolic Fontaine-Faltings module over
({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})
. For any
\alpha\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{n}
, the generic fiber of the logarithmic de Rham bundle
(V_{\alpha},\nabla_{\alpha})
is
\eta
-convergent for all
\eta\in[0,1)
.

Proof.

By the definition, the filtration in any (parabolic) Fontaine-Faltings module has level contained in
[a,b]
with
b-a\leq p-2
. The grading structure in the corresponding graded Higgs bundle
(E,\theta)
has level contained in
[0,p-2]
. in other words,
there exists graded decomposition
E=\oplus_{i=0}^{p-2}E_{i}
such that
the Higgs field is a sum of maps
E_{i}\rightarrow E_{i+1}
where
i
run through
\{0,1,\cdots,p-3\}
. We consider the modulo
p
-reduction of the connection in the Fontaine-Faltings module, which comes from the modulo
p
reduction of the graded Higgs bundle under the inverse Cartier functor. From the explicit construction of inverse Cartier functor
8
8

8

Note that the inverse Cartier functor
C_{1}^{-1}
(the characteristic
p
case) is introduced in the seminal work of Ogus-Vologodsky
[
OV07
]
. See also
[
LSZ19
]
.
, one has
\left(\nabla_{\partial}\right)^{p-1}\equiv 0\pmod{p}
. Thus the Lemma follows the definition of
\eta
-convergent in
[
Ked07
, Definition 2.4.2]
immediately.
∎

Corollary 3.15
.

Let
(V,\nabla,\Fil,\varphi)
be a parabolic Fontaine-Faltings module over
({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})
. For any
\alpha\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{n}
, the generic fiber of the logarithmic de Rham bundle
(V_{\alpha},\nabla_{\alpha})
is convergent.

Proof.

The convergence follows
subsubsection 3.3.2
, by Kedlaya’s criterion
[
Ked07
, 2.5.6]
.
∎

Together with Kedlaya’s equivalent functor
[
Ked07
, 6.4.1]
, we get functors from the category logarithmic Fontaine-Faltings modules
({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{Y}})
to the category convergent logarithmic isocrystal over
(Y_{1},D_{1})
indexed by
\alpha\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{n}
.

(3.3)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\text{parabolic Fontaine-Faltings}\\
\text{modules over $({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}})$}\\
\end{array}\right\}\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}\left\{{\text{
convergent logarithmic
}\atop\text{
isocrystal over $(Y_{1},D_{Y_{1}})$
}}\right\}

Generally, we get a Frobenius structure on these convergent logarithmic isocrystal over
(Y_{1},D_{Y_{1}})
.

Proposition 3.16
.

Let
(V,\nabla,\Fil,\varphi)
be a parabolic Fontaine-Faltings module over
({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{Y}})
. Let
\{{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}\}
be the associated convergent logarithmic isocrystals over
(Y_{1},D_{1})
given in (
3.3
). Then

(1)

the de Rham bundles
F^{*}(\widetilde{V}_{\alpha},\widetilde{\nabla}_{\alpha})
are convergent for all
\alpha\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{n}
, and they have common restriction on the open subset
{\mathcal{Y}}_{K}^{\circ}
.

(2)

The Frobenius structure in parabolic Fontaine-Faltings module induces an natural injective morphism of logarithmic de Rham bundles over
({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{Y}})

\varphi\colon F^{*}(\widetilde{V}_{0},\widetilde{\nabla}_{0})\hookrightarrow(V_{0},\nabla_{0}).

(3)

After restricting
{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}
onto the open subset
{\mathcal{Y}}_{K}^{\circ}
, one gets an overconvergent
F
-isocrystal over
(U_{1},Y_{1})
. In summary, we gets a functor

(3.4)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\text{parabolic Fontaine-Faltings}\\
\text{modules over $({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}})$}\\
\end{array}\right\}\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}\left\{{\text{
overconvergent
}\atop\text{
$F$-isocrystal over $(U_{1},Y_{1})$
}}\right\}

Proof.

Clearly, (1) and (3) follows (2) directly. We show (2) as follows.

Since one always has injection
(V_{0},\nabla_{0},\Fil_{0})\hookrightarrow(V,\nabla,\Fil)
of filtered parabolic de Rham bundles, the front one endowed with trivial parabolic structure. After taking parabolic version of Faltings tilde functor and inverse Cartier functor, one gets an injective morphism between parabolic de Rham bundles

{\mathcal{F}}^{*}(\widetilde{V}_{0},\widetilde{\nabla}_{0})\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{F}}^{*}(\widetilde{V},\widetilde{\nabla}).

Then composing with the Frobenius structure in the Fontaine-Faltings module, one gets the desired injective morphism.
∎

Remark 3.17
.

Due to the existence of the parabolic structure, the Frobenius map in (2) is not isomorphism in general. But if the parabolic structure is trivial(in other word, for a logarithmic Fontaine-Faltings module), we will indeed get a convergent logarithmic
F
-isocrystal over
(U_{1},Y_{1})
.

We now have endomorphism structures involved.

Corollary 3.18
.

By forgetting the filtration, and then restricting on the Raynaud generic fiber, one gets the following functor

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\text{parabolic Fontaine-Faltings}\\
\text{modules over $({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}})/S$ with}\\
\text{${\mathbb{Z}}_{p^{f}}$-endomorphism structures}\\
\end{array}\right\}\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}\left\{{\text{
overconvergent $F$-isocrystal over
}\atop\text{
$(Y_{1},D_{Y_{1}})$ with coefficient in ${\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{f}}$
}}\right\}

3.4.
Overconvergent
F
-isocrystals on the projective line

3.4.1.
Overconvergent
F
-isocrystals with given exponents

Denote by
{\FIsoc^{\dagger{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k)_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{f}}}
the set of all rank-
2
overconvergent
F
-isocrystal
{\mathcal{E}}
over
({\mathbb{P}}^{1}_{k},\{0,1,\lambda,\infty\})
with coefficients in
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{f}}
such that the exponents along
0,1,\lambda
are integers and the exponents along
\infty
are half integers.

Let
k^{\prime}
be a field extension of
k
containing
{\mathbb{F}}_{p^{f}}
. For any
M\in\mathcal{MF}^{{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}(W(k^{\prime}))_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p^{f}}}
, by
subsubsection 3.3.2
, we get an overconvergent
F
-isocrystal
{\mathcal{E}}_{M}
over
({\mathbb{P}}^{1}_{k^{\prime}},\{0,1,\lambda,\infty\})/k^{\prime}
endowed with an
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{f}}
-endomorphism structure with the same exponents as
M
(up to modulo
{\mathbb{Z}}
). Thus
{\mathcal{E}}_{M}\in{\FIsoc^{\dagger{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k^{\prime})_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{f}}}
. This give us a
natural map

(3.5)

\mathcal{MF}^{{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}(W(k^{\prime}))_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p^{f}}}\rightarrow{\FIsoc^{\dagger{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k^{\prime})_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{f}}}.

3.4.2.
An equivalence relation on
{\FIsoc^{\dagger{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k^{\prime})_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{f}}}

Let
k^{\prime}
be a field extension of
k
containing
{\mathbb{F}}_{p^{f}}
.

Definition 3.19
.

Let
{\mathcal{E}}
and
{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}\in{\FIsoc^{\dagger{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k^{\prime})_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{f}}}
. We call they are
differed by a constant (over
k^{\prime}
)
, if there exists an
F
-isocrystal
{\mathcal{E}}^{\circ}
over
k^{\prime}
with coefficient in
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{f}}
of rank
1
such that

{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}={\mathcal{E}}\otimes{\mathcal{E}}^{\circ}.

Differed by a constant is an equivalent relation on the set

{\FIsoc^{\dagger{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k^{\prime})_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{f}}}.

Denote by
[{\FIsoc^{\dagger{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k^{\prime})_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{f}}}]
the set of all equivalent classes.

Denote by
{\FIsoc^{\dagger{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k^{\prime})_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{f}}}^{\rm triv}
the subset of
{\FIsoc^{\dagger{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k^{\prime})_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{f}}}
with trivial determinant, and denote by
[{\FIsoc^{\dagger{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k^{\prime})_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{f}}}^{\rm triv}]
the image of
{\FIsoc^{\dagger{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k^{\prime})_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{f}}}^{\rm triv}
in
[{\FIsoc^{\dagger{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k^{\prime})_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{f}}}]
.

Lemma 3.20
.

Let
{\mathcal{E}}
and
{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}\in{\FIsoc^{\dagger{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k^{\prime})_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{f}}}
. Then for any
n\geq 1
, they are differed by a constant over
k_{n}^{\prime}
if and only if they are differed by a constant over
k^{\prime}
. Thus one has the natural injection

[{\FIsoc^{\dagger{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k^{\prime})_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{f}}}]\hookrightarrow[{\FIsoc^{\dagger{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k^{\prime}_{n})_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{f}}}].

Lemma 3.21
.

The map (
3.5
) induces an injection between the sets of equivalence classes

[\mathcal{MF}^{{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}(W(k^{\prime}))_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p^{f}}}]\hookrightarrow[{\FIsoc^{\dagger{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k^{\prime})_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{f}}}].

Proof.

This follows the facts that the modulo
p
reductions of the de Rham terms appeared in the Higgs-de Rham flow associating to any object in
\mathcal{MF}^{{1\over 2}}_{\lambda,f}(W(k^{\prime}))
are all stable and the Hodge filtration is unique.
∎

Lemma 3.22
.

Assume
2\mid f
. Then the map in
subsubsection 3.4.2
(replacing
k^{\prime}
with
k^{\prime}_{2}
) induces following injection

[\mathcal{MF}^{{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}(W(k_{2}^{\prime}))_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p^{f}}}^{\rm cy}]\hookrightarrow[{\FIsoc^{\dagger{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k^{\prime}_{2})_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{f}}}^{\rm triv}].

Proof.

For any
M\in\mathcal{MF}^{{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}(W(k^{\prime}))_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p^{f}}}^{\rm cy}
, the
F
-isocrystal
{\mathcal{E}}_{M}\otimes{\mathcal{E}}_{1/2}^{-1}
has trivial determinant.
∎

Together with natural mapping from periodic Higgs bundles to Fontaine-Faltings modules, we get the following result.

Corollary 3.23
.

Assume
\lambda\in W(k)
is supersingular. Running Higgs-de Rham flow induces a natural injection

(3.6)

{\mathrm{PHIG}^{{\rm gr}\,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda,f}}(k^{\prime})\hookrightarrow[{\FIsoc^{\dagger{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k^{\prime})_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{f}}}].

3.4.3.
The Frobenius action on
{\FIsoc^{\dagger{1\over 2}}_{\lambda,f}}(k)

Proposition 3.24
.

Assume
\lambda\in W(k)
is supersingular.
The injection in (
3.6
) is preserved by the actions of
\mathrm{Frob}_{k}
on both sides.

Proof.

Let
(E,\theta)\in{\mathrm{PHIG}^{{\rm gr}\,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda,f}}(k^{\prime})
with corresponding
F
-isocrystal
{\mathcal{E}}\in{\FIsoc^{\dagger{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k^{\prime})_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{f}}}
. Assume it initials the following periodic Higgs-de Rham flow

\mathrm{Flow}=\{(E,\theta)_{0}=(E,\theta),(V,\nabla,\Fil)_{0},(E,\theta)_{1},(V,\nabla,\Fil)_{1},\cdots\}.

Since
\lambda
is supersingular, it lifts uniquely (up to a constant) to an
f
-periodic flow in
\mathrm{HDF}^{{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}(W(k^{\prime}))
.

\widehat{\mathrm{Flow}}=(\widehat{E},\widehat{\theta})_{0},(\widehat{V},\widehat{\nabla},\widehat{\Fil})_{0},(\widehat{E},\widehat{\theta})_{1},(\widehat{V},\widehat{\nabla},\widehat{\Fil})_{1},\cdots.

Then by forgetting the Frobenius structure in Fontaine-Faltings module, one gets the underlying filtered de Rham bundle

(\widehat{V},\widehat{\nabla},\widehat{\Fil})=(\widehat{V},\widehat{\nabla},\widehat{\Fil})_{0}\oplus(\widehat{V},\widehat{\nabla},\widehat{\Fil})_{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus(\widehat{V},\widehat{\nabla},\widehat{\Fil})_{f-1}

with a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p^{f}}
-endomorphism structure
\iota
. We note that due to the existence of the Frobenius structure we have

\Gr((\widehat{V},\widehat{\nabla},\widehat{\Fil})_{f-1})\simeq(\widehat{E},\widehat{\theta})_{0}.

Thus we can reconstruct
(E,\theta)
from the
0
-th eigen component
(\widehat{V},\widehat{\nabla})_{f-1}
.

By the construction of
{\mathcal{E}}
, its underlying de Rham bundle is just the generic fiber
(\widehat{V}_{K},\widehat{\nabla}_{K})
. We can also taking the
0
-th eigen component, which is just
(\widehat{V}_{K},\widehat{\nabla}_{K})_{f-1}
. Since the modulo
p
-reduction of
(\widehat{V},\widehat{\nabla})_{f-1}
is stable, up to isomorphism
(\widehat{V}_{K},\widehat{\nabla}_{K})
has a unique integral extension, which is just
(\widehat{V},\widehat{\nabla})_{f-1}
.

In summary, from
{\mathcal{E}}
we can reconstruct the Higgs bundle as follows:

•

find the
0
-th eigen component of underlying de Rham bundle of
{\mathcal{E}}
;

•

find an integral extension of the de Rham bundle in the first step.

•

take grading (the Hodge filtration is unique, due to
subsection 2.1
) and modulo
p
, one gets the original Higgs bundle
(E,\theta)
.

Now starting from
\mathrm{Frob}_{k}({\mathcal{E}})
and following steps as above, one then get the Higgs bundle
\mathrm{Frob}_{k}(E,\theta)
.
∎

Remark 3.25
.

Due to the existence of the Hodge filtration, there is no Frobenius action on the intermediate sets
[\mathrm{PHDF}^{{1\over 2}}_{\lambda,f}(W(k_{f}))]
and
[\mathcal{MF}^{{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}(W(k))_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p^{f}}}]
. If one forgets the Hodge filtrations in Fontaine-Faltings modules, then he will get some “parabolic
F
-crystals”, on which there should exist an action of
\mathrm{Frob}_{k}
. And the natural maps between them should preserves the Frobenius structure.

4.
p
-to-
\ell
companion

In this section, the aim is to construct the injections

(4.1)

\textstyle{[{\FIsoc^{\dagger{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k^{\prime}_{2})_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{f}}}^{\rm triv}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
subsubsection 4.3.1
\textstyle{[{\mathrm{LOC}^{\ell,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k^{\prime}_{2})]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
subsubsection 4.2.2
\textstyle{{\mathrm{LOC}^{\ell,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(\overline{k})^{\mathrm{Frob}_{k_{2}^{\prime}}}}

And show that the image of the following composition

(4.2)

\textstyle{{\mathrm{HIG}^{{\rm gr}\,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
3.2
)
\textstyle{[{\FIsoc^{\dagger{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k^{\prime}_{2})_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{f}}}^{\rm triv}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
4.1
)
\textstyle{{\mathrm{LOC}^{\ell,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(\overline{k})^{\mathrm{Frob}_{k_{2}^{\prime}}}}

is contained in
{\mathrm{LOC}^{\ell,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(\overline{k})^{\mathrm{Frob}_{k}}
, see
subsubsection 4.3.1
. Finially, Yu’s formular(
Theorem 4.7
) for a numeric Simpson correspondence implies that the composition in (
4.2
) is a genuine Simpson correspondence

{\mathrm{HIG}^{{\rm gr}\,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k)\xrightarrow[\autoref{thm_genuineSimCorr}]{1:1}{\mathrm{LOC}^{\ell,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(\overline{k})^{\mathrm{Frob}_{k}}.

Here
[{\mathrm{LOC}^{\ell,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k^{\prime}_{2})]
and
{\mathrm{LOC}^{\ell,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(\overline{k})^{\mathrm{Frob}_{k_{2}^{\prime}}}
are defined in
subsubsection 4.2.1
and
subsubsection 4.2.2
. Roughly speaking,

•

[{\mathrm{LOC}^{\ell,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k^{\prime}_{2})]
is the set of isomorphic classes of rank
2
tame
\ell
-adic local systems
{\mathbb{L}}
on the punctured projective line
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}_{k_{2}^{\prime}}\setminus\{0,1,\lambda,\infty\}
with prescribed eigenvalues of the local monodromies modulo an equivalence defined in
subsubsection 4.2.2
.

•

{\mathrm{LOC}^{\ell,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(\overline{k})^{\mathrm{Frob}_{k_{2}^{\prime}}}
is the set of all isomorphic classes of rank
2
tame
\ell
-adic local systems
{\mathbb{L}}
fixed by the Frobenius
\mathrm{Frob}_{k_{2}^{\prime}}
on the punctured projective line
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}_{\overline{k}}\setminus\{0,1,\lambda,\infty\}
with prescribed eigenvalues of the local monodromies.

As a consequence, we have

Theorem 4.1
.

Suppose
\lambda
is supersingular.

(1)

The trace field of any
F
-isocrystal in
{\FIsoc^{\dagger{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k^{\prime}_{2})_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{f}}}^{\rm triv}
is unramified above
p
.

(2)

The trace field of the isocrystal attached to an Fontaine-Faltings module in
\mathcal{MF}^{{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}(W(k^{\prime}_{2}))_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p^{f}}}^{\rm cy}
is unramified.

Proof.

(2) follows from (1) and the bijection. We only need to show (1). For any
F
-isocrystal
{\mathcal{E}}\in{\FIsoc^{\dagger{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k^{\prime}_{2})_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{f}}}^{\rm triv}
, denote by
{\mathbb{E}}
the trace field and denote by
{\mathbb{L}}
the associated
\ell
-adic local system. For any
\sigma\colon\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\cong\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
, we have the
p
-adic companion
{\mathcal{E}}^{\sigma}
and
\ell
-adic companion
{\mathbb{L}}^{\sigma}
. Since taking companion preserves the eigenvalues of local monodormies,
{\mathbb{L}}^{\sigma}
is also contained in
{\mathrm{LOC}^{\ell,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(\overline{k})^{\mathrm{Frob}_{k}}
. Thus
{\mathcal{E}}^{\sigma}
is contained in
{\FIsoc^{\dagger{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k^{\prime}_{2})_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{f}}}^{\rm triv}
and
\sigma({\mathbb{E}})\subset{\mathbb{Q}}_{p^{f}}
for any
\sigma
. Hence
{\mathbb{E}}
is unramified above
p
.
∎

4.1.
\ell
-adic local systems

4.1.1.
The character
\chi_{1/2}

Recall that the Galois group
G_{{\mathbb{F}}_{p}}=\Gal(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}/{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
is isomorphic to
\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}}
with a topological generator
\sigma
. Denote by

\chi_{1/2}

the
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
-character of the subgroup
G_{{\mathbb{F}}_{p^{2}}}=\Gal(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}/{\mathbb{F}}_{p^{2}})

given by

\chi_{1/2}(\sigma^{2})=p.

Clearly
\chi_{1/2}^{2}={\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}(1)
is just the cyclotomic character.

4.1.2.
The change of characteristic polynomials under twisting by the character
\chi_{1/2}

Let
k
be a finite field with cardinality
p^{h}
containing
{\mathbb{F}}_{p^{2}}
. The character
\chi_{1/2}
can be restricted on the absolute Galois group
G_{k}
of
k
. Let
{\mathbb{L}}
be a
{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
-representation of the absolute Galois group
G_{k}
of
k
.

Denote by
P({\mathbb{L}},t)
the characteristic polynomial and by
\tr({\mathbb{L}})
the trace of
\sigma^{h}
acting on
{\mathbb{L}}
. Then

Lemma 4.2
.

let
{\mathbb{L}}
be a local system of rank
r
. Then

P({\mathbb{L}}\otimes\chi_{1/2},t)=p^{rh/2}P({\mathbb{L}},p^{-h/2}t)\quad\text{and}\quad\tr({\mathbb{L}}\otimes\chi)=p^{h/2}\tr({\mathbb{L}}).

4.2.
\ell
-adic local systems over punctured projective line and Yu’s formula

4.2.1.
\ell
-adic local systems over punctured projective line

Denote by
{\mathrm{LOC}^{\ell,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k)
the set of isomorphic classes of rank
2
tame
\ell
-adic local systems
{\mathbb{L}}
on the punctured projective line
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}_{k}\setminus\{0,1,\lambda,\infty\}
with following prescribed eigenvalues of the local monodromies:

•

the local monodromies around
\{0,1,\lambda\}
is unipotent;

•

the local monodromy around
\infty
is quasi-unipotent and has double eigenvalue
-1
.

4.2.2.
An equivalence relation on
{\mathrm{LOC}^{\ell,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k)

Definition 4.3
.

Let
{\mathbb{L}}
and
{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}\in{\mathrm{LOC}^{\ell,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k)
. We call they are differed by a character, if there exists a character
\chi
of the absolute Galois group
\Gal(\overline{k}/k)
such that

{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}={\mathbb{L}}\otimes\chi.

Denote by
[{\mathrm{LOC}^{\ell,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k)]
the set of all equivalent classes.

Lemma 4.4
.

All local systems in
{\mathrm{LOC}^{\ell,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k)
are geometrically irreducible.

Proof.

Suppose not. Then there exists some rank-1 sub local system
{\mathbb{W}}
of the geometric part of some
{\mathbb{L}}
in
{\mathrm{LOC}^{\ell,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k)
. Then the local monodromy matrix of
{\mathbb{W}}
around
\{0,1,\lambda\}
are all equal to 1, and around
\{\infty\}
is
-1
. As the four generators
\{\gamma_{0},\gamma_{1},\gamma_{\lambda},\gamma_{\infty}\}
of the geometric fundamental group of
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}\setminus\{0,1,\infty,\lambda\}
around the 4 punctures have one relation

\gamma_{0}\cdot\gamma_{1},\cdot\gamma_{\lambda}\cdot\gamma_{\infty}=e

we obtain

1\cdot 1\cdot 1\cdot(-1)=1,

which leads a contradiction.
∎

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
{\mathbb{L}}
and
{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}\in{\mathrm{LOC}^{\ell,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k)
. Then
{\mathbb{L}}
and
{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}
are differed by a character if and only if they have isomorphic geometric parts (i.e., restrictions on
U_{\overline{k}}
).

Proof.

The “only if” part is trivial. Suppose there have the same geometric parts. Let
\rho
and
\rho^{\prime}
be the representations of
\pi_{1}(U_{k})
associated to two equivalent local systems
{\mathbb{L}}
and
{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}
. Then by assumption

\rho\mid_{\pi_{1}(U_{\overline{k}})}=\rho^{\prime}\mid_{\pi_{1}(U_{\overline{k}})}.

Assume
\gamma\in\Gal(\overline{k}/k)
. Then for any two lifting
\widehat{\gamma}
and
\widehat{\gamma}^{\prime}
of
\gamma
in
\pi_{1}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}_{k}\setminus\{0,1,\lambda,\infty\})
,
\widehat{\gamma}^{-1}\cdot\widehat{\gamma}^{\prime}\in\pi_{1}(U_{\overline{k}})
. So
\rho(\widehat{\gamma}^{-1}\cdot\widehat{\gamma}^{\prime})=\rho^{\prime}(\widehat{\gamma}^{-1}\cdot\widehat{\gamma}^{\prime})
. This implies

\rho^{\prime}(\widehat{\gamma})\cdot\rho(\widehat{\gamma})^{-1}=\rho^{\prime}(\widehat{\gamma}^{\prime})\cdot\rho(\widehat{\gamma}^{\prime})^{-1}

In other words, the value
\rho^{\prime}(\widehat{\gamma}^{\prime})\cdot\rho(\widehat{\gamma}^{\prime})^{-1}
does not depend on the choice of the lifting, denote it by
\chi(\gamma)
. We only need to show that
\chi
is a character of
\Gal(\overline{k}/k)
.

Since
\pi_{1}(U_{\overline{k}})
is a normal subgroup of
\pi_{1}(U_{k})
,
\widehat{\gamma}^{-1}g\widehat{\gamma}\in\pi_{1}(U_{\overline{k}})
for any
g\in\pi_{1}(U_{\overline{k}})
. So
\rho^{\prime}(\widehat{\gamma}^{-1}g\widehat{\gamma})=\rho(\widehat{\gamma}^{-1}g\widehat{\gamma})
and
\rho^{\prime}(g)=\rho(g)
. Thus

\chi(\gamma)\rho(g)=\rho(g)\chi(\gamma).

By Schur’s lemma,

(4.3)

\chi(\gamma)\in\End(\rho\mid_{\pi_{1}(U_{\overline{k}})})\cong{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}.

Next, we only need to show
\chi
is multiplicative. For any two elements
\gamma_{1}
and
\gamma_{2}
in
\Gal(\overline{k}/k)
, choose liftings
\widehat{\gamma}_{1}
and
\widehat{\gamma}_{2}
respectively. Then

\begin{split}\chi(\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}):=&\rho^{\prime}(\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2})\cdot\rho(\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2})^{-1}\\
=&\rho^{\prime}(\gamma_{1})\cdot\chi(\gamma_{2})\cdot\rho(\gamma_{1})^{-1}\\
\overset{\eqref{equ:schur}}{=}&\rho^{\prime}(\gamma_{1})\cdot\rho(\gamma_{1})^{-1}\cdot\chi(\gamma_{2})\\
=&\chi(\gamma_{1})\cdot\chi(\gamma_{2}).\qed\end{split}

Corollary 4.6
.

One has an injection

[{\mathrm{LOC}^{\ell,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k)]\hookrightarrow{\mathrm{LOC}^{\ell,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(\overline{k})^{\mathrm{Frob}_{k}}.

4.2.3.
Yu’s formula

By Drinfeld and Deligne the set of
{\mathrm{LOC}^{\ell,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(\overline{k})^{\mathrm{Frob}_{k}}
is finite. However, it is not clear that how the number depends on
q:=\#k
precisely. Very recently Hongjie Yu
[
Yu23
]
has solved Deligne’s conjecture on counting
\ell
-adic local systems in terms of parabolic Higgs bundles. His general theorem applying to our special case turns out:

Theorem 4.7

(Hongjie Yu
[
Yu23
]
)
.

\#{\mathrm{HIG}^{{\rm gr}\,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k)=\#{\mathrm{LOC}^{\ell,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(\overline{k})^{\mathrm{Frob}_{k}}.

Remark 4.8
.

When
\lambda
is supersingular, we will show this numeric Simpson correspondence in fact underlies a genuine Simpson correspondence, see
subsubsection 4.3.1
.

4.3.

p
-to-
\ell
companion

In this subsection, we choose a prime
\ell\not=p
and fix an isomorphism
\phi\colon\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}\simeq\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
. Our aim is to construct a natural injection

{\mathrm{HIG}^{{\rm gr}\,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(k)\hookrightarrow{\mathrm{LOC}^{\ell,{1\over 2}}_{\lambda}}(\overline{k})^{\mathrm{Frob}_{k}}.

The construction is diagrammatically sketched below:
